<a id='question-061'></a>

### Question 61: Anaphylaxis and Leukocytosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Epinephrine administration causes transient leukocytosis due to the physical demargination of neutrophils from the vascular endothelium (肾上腺素诱导的瞬时白细胞增多症，是由于中性粒细胞从血管内皮边缘物理脱落造成的).

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 16-year-old male with a known severe peanut allergy is eating at a restaurant when he accidentally ingests a peanut-containing dish. Within minutes, he develops diffuse urticaria, angioedema of the lips, wheezing, and shortness of breath. Emergency medical services are called, and he is immediately given a 0.3 mg intramuscular injection of epinephrine in the thigh. His vital signs stabilize, and his respiratory distress improves significantly. A complete blood count (CBC) is drawn 20 minutes after the epinephrine administration. The CBC reveals a total white blood cell (WBC) count of 15,200/uL (normal range: 4,500-11,000/uL), with a differential showing 85% segmented neutrophils, 5% lymphocytes, 3% monocytes, 4% eosinophils, and 3% basophils. Which of the following mechanisms best explains this finding?

(A) Epinephrine stimulates the bone marrow to release preformed neutrophils into the circulation.
(B) Epinephrine induces apoptosis of neutrophils, leading to a decrease in circulating neutrophils and a compensatory increase in their release from the bone marrow.
(C) Epinephrine causes increased vascular permeability, leading to the extravasation of neutrophils into the interstitial space, resulting in a relative increase in the concentration of neutrophils in the circulating blood.
(D) Epinephrine stimulates beta-2 adrenergic receptors on endothelial cells, causing them to contract and physically push marginated neutrophils into the central lumen of blood vessels.
(E) Epinephrine stimulates the release of cortisol from the adrenal cortex, which promotes neutrophil production and release from the bone marrow.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient:** 16-year-old male with peanut allergy. This sets the stage for anaphylaxis.
- **Event:** Accidental peanut ingestion leading to anaphylaxis symptoms (urticaria, angioedema, wheezing, shortness of breath). This confirms the clinical context.
- **Intervention:** Intramuscular epinephrine injection. This is the key stimulus for the observed change.
- **Timing:** CBC drawn 20 minutes after epinephrine. This timeframe is crucial for understanding the mechanism (transient effect).
- **Lab Finding:** Elevated WBC count (15,200/uL) with neutrophilia (85% segmented neutrophils). This is the core observation to explain.
- **Question:** Asks for the mechanism explaining the leukocytosis.

The key is the rapid onset of neutrophilia following epinephrine administration in the context of anaphylaxis. This points towards a mobilization of a pre-existing pool of neutrophils rather than increased production or decreased destruction.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Neutrol Margination:** Neutrophils normally circulate in the bloodstream, but a significant portion (estimated 30-50%) adheres loosely to the endothelial lining of post-capillary venules and capillaries, forming a "marginated pool." This pool serves as a readily available reserve.
- **Epinephrine Action:** Epinephrine, particularly via beta-2 adrenergic receptor stimulation, causes vasoconstriction in some vascular beds and vasodilation in others. Crucially, it increases blood flow velocity and shear stress within vessels.
- **Physical Demargination:** The increased shear stress generated by the increased blood flow physically dislodges the marginated neutrophils from the endothelium. This process is called "demargination."
- **Result:** The neutrophils from the marginated pool rapidly enter the central circulation, leading to a transient increase in the total circulating WBC count, primarily composed of neutrophils. This effect is typically observed within minutes of epinephrine administration and lasts for about 30-60 minutes.
- **Clinical Context:** Epinephrine is used in anaphylaxis not only for its bronchodilator and vasoconstrictor effects but also because this demargination effect can transiently increase the number of circulating neutrophils, potentially aiding in combating infection or inflammation, although this is not its primary therapeutic role in anaphylaxis.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Epinephrine does not directly stimulate the bone marrow to release neutrophils acutely. Bone marrow stimulation takes hours to days to significantly increase neutrophil production and release (e.g., in response to G-CSF or infection). The observed leukocytosis is too rapid to be due to bone marrow release.
- (B): **Incorrect.** Epinephrine does not induce neutrophil apoptosis. Apoptosis would lead to a *decrease* in circulating neutrophils, not an increase. Furthermore, the leukocytosis is transient, not a result of compensatory release after apoptosis.
- (C): **Incorrect.** Epinephrine causes vasoconstriction in some areas, which would *decrease* vascular permeability, not increase it. Increased vascular permeability leads to extravasation (movement out of vessels) into the interstitial space, which would *decrease* the concentration of neutrophils in the circulating blood, not increase it.
- (E): **Incorrect.** While epinephrine administration does stimulate the adrenal cortex to release cortisol, cortisol's effect on neutrophil production and release from the bone marrow is a slower process, taking hours to days, similar to option (A). It does not explain the rapid, transient leukocytosis observed 20 minutes after epinephrine injection. Cortisol primarily affects neutrophil function (e.g., increasing their numbers in circulation over longer periods) rather than causing acute demargination.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Epinephrine administration causes a rapid, transient leukocytosis primarily due to the physical demargination of neutrophils from the vascular endothelium. This is a key physiological effect mediated by beta-2 adrenergic stimulation and increased shear stress. Remember this mechanism for questions involving epinephrine use, particularly in emergency situations like anaphylaxis. Differential diagnoses for leukocytosis include infection, inflammation, stress, malignancy, and certain medications (e.g., corticosteroids). However, the specific context of epinephrine administration points strongly to demargination.

---

<a id='question-062'></a>

### Question 62: Pneumococcal Sialidase and Neutrophil Recruitment
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Bacterial neuraminidase (sialidase) produced by *Streptococcus pneumoniae* cleaves sialic acid residues from cell surface glycoproteins, specifically Sialyl Lewis X, thereby inhibiting neutrophil rolling and adhesion to endothelial cells, representing a mechanism of immune evasion. (细菌肺炎链球菌产生的神经氨酸酶（唾液酸酶）切割细胞表面糖蛋白上的唾液酸残基，特别是唾液基Lewis X，从而抑制中性粒细胞在内皮细胞上的滚动和粘附，这是一种免疫逃避机制。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 68-year-old male with a history of chronic obstructive pulmonary disease (COPD) and recent influenza infection presents to the emergency department with acute onset of high fever (39.5°C), productive cough with purulent sputum, and pleuritic chest pain. His respiratory rate is 32 breaths/min, and oxygen saturation is 88% on room air. Chest X-ray reveals a right lower lobe consolidation. Sputum Gram stain shows Gram-positive diplococci. A clinical isolate of the causative organism is cultured and found to produce a potent extracellular enzyme. In vitro studies using human neutrophils demonstrate that pre-treatment of neutrophils with this enzyme significantly reduces their ability to bind to endothelial cells expressing P-selectin and E-selectin, impairing their rolling along the endothelium.

Which of the following mechanisms best explains how this bacterial enzyme contributes to the pathogenesis of pneumococcal pneumonia?

(A) Inhibition of complement activation by preventing C3b deposition on the bacterial surface.
(B) Degradation of surfactant proteins, leading to alveolar collapse and impaired gas exchange.
(C) Cleavage of sialic acid residues from Sialyl Lewis X on neutrophils, preventing their adhesion to endothelial selectins.
(D) Production of a capsule composed of hyaluronic acid, which inhibits phagocytosis by macrophages.
(E) Release of endotoxin (lipopolysaccharide), triggering a massive systemic inflammatory response and septic shock.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 68-year-old male, COPD, recent influenza, acute fever, productive cough (purulent sputum), pleuritic chest pain, tachypnea, hypoxia, right lower lobe consolidation on CXR. This strongly suggests bacterial pneumonia, likely secondary to influenza.
- **Microbiology:** Sputum Gram stain shows Gram-positive diplococci, highly suggestive of *Streptococcus pneumoniae*.
- **Bacterial Enzyme:** The clinical isolate produces a potent extracellular enzyme.
- **In Vitro Findings:** Pre-treatment of neutrophils with this enzyme reduces their binding to endothelial cells expressing P-selectin and E-selectin, impairing rolling. This points to a mechanism involving neutrophil adhesion and endothelial interactions.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism by which the bacterial enzyme contributes to pathogenesis.

The key clues are the Gram-positive diplococci (*S. pneumoniae*), the production of an extracellular enzyme, and the specific effect of this enzyme on neutrophil rolling mediated by P-selectin and E-selectin. This combination points directly to the role of pneumococcal sialidase (neuraminidase).

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
*Streptococcus pneumoniae* is a common cause of community-acquired pneumonia, especially following viral infections like influenza. A key virulence factor is its polysaccharide capsule, which inhibits phagocytosis. However, the vignette focuses on an extracellular enzyme that impairs neutrophil recruitment.
1.  **Inflammation:** Bacterial infection triggers an inflammatory response in the lung parenchyma.
2.  **Neutrophil Recruitment:** Neutrophils are crucial for clearing bacterial infections. Their recruitment involves several steps:
    *   **Rolling:** Neutrophils loosely bind to the endothelial surface via selectins (E-selectin, P-selectin) expressed on activated endothelial cells. This binding is mediated by carbohydrate ligands on the neutrophil surface.
    *   **Activation:** Chemokines activate neutrophils, causing conformational changes in integrins (e.g., LFA-1).
    *   **Adhesion:** Activated integrins bind tightly to their ligands (e.g., ICAM-1) on endothelial cells, causing the neutrophil to firmly adhere.
    *   **Transmigration (Diapedesis):** Neutrophils squeeze between endothelial cells and migrate into the infected tissue.
3.  **Sialyl Lewis X:** The primary ligand for selectins on neutrophils is Sialyl Lewis X (sLex), a carbohydrate structure containing sialic acid (N-acetylneuraminic acid, Neu5Ac).
4.  **Pneumococcal Sialidase (Neuraminidase):** *S. pneumoniae* produces an extracellular enzyme called sialidase or neuraminidase. This enzyme specifically cleaves terminal sialic acid residues from glycoproteins.
5.  **Mechanism of Action:** The sialidase cleaves the sialic acid moiety from Sialyl Lewis X on the surface of neutrophils. This removes the ligand required for binding to P-selectin and E-selectin.
6.  **Consequence:** Neutrophils treated with pneumococcal sialidase lose their ability to roll along the endothelium expressing P-selectin and E-selectin. This impairs the initial step of neutrophil recruitment to the site of infection.
7.  **Immune Evasion:** By inhibiting neutrophil rolling, the sialidase acts as a virulence factor, allowing the bacteria to evade the host's immune response and persist in the lung tissue, contributing to the severity of the pneumonia.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Inhibition of complement activation by preventing C3b deposition on the bacterial surface.** While *S. pneumoniae* capsule can interfere with complement activation (especially the alternative pathway) and opsonization, the primary mechanism described in the vignette involves neutrophil rolling mediated by selectins, not complement inhibition. The enzyme's specific effect on Sialyl Lewis X is the key clue pointing away from this option.
- (B): **Degradation of surfactant proteins, leading to alveolar collapse and impaired gas exchange.** Some bacteria can produce proteases that degrade surfactant, but this is not the mechanism described for the specific enzyme in the vignette, which targets neutrophil adhesion via selectins. The vignette focuses on the enzyme's effect on neutrophils, not surfactant.
- (D): **Production of a capsule composed of hyaluronic acid, which inhibits phagocytosis by macrophages.** *S. pneumoniae* capsule is primarily composed of polysaccharides (e.g., capsular serotypes), not hyaluronic acid. Hyaluronic acid capsules are characteristic of *Streptococcus pyogenes*. The capsule of *S. pneumoniae* inhibits phagocytosis, but the vignette describes the action of a specific *enzyme* affecting neutrophil rolling, not the capsule's anti-phagocytic role.
- (E): **Release of endotoxin (lipopolysaccharide), triggering a massive systemic inflammatory response and septic shock.** Endotoxin (LPS) is characteristic of Gram-negative bacteria, not Gram-positive bacteria like *S. pneumoniae*. *S. pneumoniae* can cause sepsis, but it is not mediated by endotoxin release. The vignette specifically describes the action of an extracellular enzyme on neutrophil adhesion.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
*Streptococcus pneumoniae* utilizes sialidase (neuraminidase) to cleave Sialyl Lewis X on neutrophils, inhibiting their rolling on endothelial selectins (P-selectin, E-selectin) and impairing neutrophil recruitment to the site of infection. This is a key immune evasion strategy. Remember that selectin ligands are typically sialylated carbohydrates.
Differential diagnosis includes other bacterial virulence factors affecting neutrophil recruitment (e.g., toxins inhibiting integrin activation) or complement evasion (e.g., capsules). Also, recall that endotoxin is specific to Gram-negative bacteria.

<a id='question-063'></a>

### Question 63: Sickle Cell Disease Management
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The mechanism of action of crizanlizumab in preventing vaso-occlusive crises in sickle cell disease by targeting P-selectin-mediated adhesion. (克里扎利珠单抗在镰状细胞疾病中通过靶向 P 选择素介导的粘附作用预防血管闭塞危机的机制)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 21-year-old African American male with a known history of homozygous sickle cell anemia (HbSS) presents for a follow-up appointment. He reports experiencing five painful vaso-occlusive crises (VOCs) requiring emergency department visits and opioid analgesia over the past year. To reduce the frequency of these debilitating episodes, he was started on monthly intravenous infusions of a novel therapeutic agent 12 months ago. Since initiating this treatment, he has experienced a 45% reduction in the frequency of his VOCs. The therapeutic agent is a humanized monoclonal antibody specifically designed to bind to P-selectin, an adhesion molecule expressed on activated endothelial cells and platelets. Which of the following mechanisms best explains the therapeutic effect of this agent in reducing vaso-occlusive crises?

(A) Inhibition of sickle hemoglobin polymerization within erythrocytes.
(B) Increased production of fetal hemoglobin (HbF), which reduces erythrocyte sickling.
(C) Decreased expression of vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF), leading to reduced angiogenesis and inflammation.
(D) Prevention of adhesion between sickle erythrocytes and leukocytes to the vascular endothelium.
(E) Enhanced activity of the reticuloendothelial system, leading to increased clearance of damaged sickle cells.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 21-year-old male with homozygous sickle cell anemia (HbSS). This establishes the underlying disease characterized by abnormal hemoglobin leading to erythrocyte sickling and vaso-occlusion.
- **Clinical Problem:** Frequent vaso-occlusive crises (VOCs), a hallmark complication of sickle cell disease.
- **Intervention:** Monthly infusions of a humanized monoclonal antibody targeting P-selectin. This is the key piece of information about the specific drug (crizanlizumab).
- **Outcome:** 45% reduction in VOC frequency. This confirms the drug's efficacy.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the mechanism of action by which this P-selectin-targeting antibody reduces VOCs.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Sickle Cell Disease Pathophysiology:** HbSS leads to polymerization of deoxygenated hemoglobin, causing erythrocytes to deform into a sickle shape. These rigid, sickled cells obstruct microvascular blood flow, leading to ischemia, pain (VOCs), and tissue damage.
- **Role of Adhesion:** A critical step in VOCs is the adhesion of sickled erythrocytes and leukocytes to the activated vascular endothelium. This adhesion is mediated by various adhesion molecules.
- **P-Selectin's Role:** P-selectin is an adhesion molecule rapidly expressed on the surface of activated endothelial cells and platelets in response to inflammatory stimuli and cellular stress (like hypoxia and sickled cells themselves). It binds to its ligand, P-selectin glycoprotein ligand-1 (PSGL-1), which is expressed on leukocytes and sickle erythrocytes.
- **Crizanlizumab Mechanism:** Crizanlizumab is a humanized monoclonal antibody that specifically binds to P-selectin. By binding to P-selectin on activated endothelial cells, it sterically hinders the interaction between P-selectin and its ligand (PSGL-1) on sickle erythrocytes and leukocytes.
- **Therapeutic Effect:** Blocking this interaction prevents the firm adhesion of sickled cells and leukocytes to the endothelium, reducing the likelihood of microvascular obstruction and subsequent vaso-occlusive crises. This directly explains the observed reduction in VOC frequency.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Inhibition of sickle hemoglobin polymerization. This is the mechanism of action for drugs like hydroxyurea (indirectly, by increasing HbF) and L-glutamine. While preventing polymerization is crucial in sickle cell disease, crizanlizumab does not directly affect hemoglobin polymerization. It acts downstream, targeting the adhesion process.
- (B): Increased production of fetal hemoglobin (HbF). HbF inhibits sickle hemoglobin polymerization. Hydroxyurea is the primary drug used to increase HbF levels. Crizanlizumab does not affect HbF production.
- (C): Decreased expression of vascular endothelial growth factor (VEGF). VEGF promotes angiogenesis and vascular permeability. While inflammation and angiogenesis play roles in sickle cell complications, crizanlizumab's primary target is P-selectin, not VEGF. Drugs targeting VEGF are used in other conditions (e.g., cancer, macular degeneration).
- (E): Enhanced activity of the reticuloendothelial system. The reticuloendothelial system (mononuclear phagocyte system) removes damaged or senescent red blood cells. While increased hemolysis occurs in sickle cell disease, enhancing the reticuloendothelial system is not the mechanism of crizanlizumab. In fact, chronic hemolysis can lead to splenic dysfunction.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
Crizanlizumab is a P-selectin inhibitor used to reduce the frequency of VOCs in sickle cell disease. Its mechanism involves blocking the adhesion of sickled erythrocytes and leukocytes to the activated endothelium, thereby preventing microvascular occlusion. Key differentials for sickle cell treatments include hydroxyurea (increases HbF), L-glutamine (improves erythrocyte hydration/antioxidant), voxelotor (increases Hb affinity for oxygen), and pain management strategies. Understanding the specific molecular targets is crucial for distinguishing these therapies.

<a id='question-064'></a>

### Question 64: Leukocyte Adhesion & Post-Translational Modification
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of post-translational modifications, specifically sulfation catalyzed by tyrosylprotein sulfotransferase (TPST), and its crucial role in the function of leukocyte adhesion molecules, particularly PSGL-1 binding to P-selectin. (考察翻译后修饰，特别是由酪氨酸蛋白硫代转移酶 (TPST) 催化的硫酸化，以及它在白细胞粘附分子功能中的关键作用，特别是 PSGL-1 与 P-选择素的结合。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A research team is investigating the molecular mechanisms underlying leukocyte rolling on activated endothelial cells. They perform biochemical experiments using cultured human endothelial cells and neutrophils. They discover that a specific post-translational modification is essential for the interaction between P-selectin, expressed on activated endothelial cells, and PSGL-1 (P-selectin glycoprotein ligand-1), expressed on neutrophils. To investigate this modification, they treat neutrophils with chlorate, a known inhibitor of sulfation enzymes. They then synthesize PSGL-1 in vitro and analyze its ability to bind P-selectin under physiological flow conditions. They find that PSGL-1 synthesized in the presence of chlorate retains its Sialyl Lewis X glycosylation but completely loses its ability to bind P-selectin. Further experiments confirm that the enzyme responsible for this modification is tyrosylprotein sulfotransferase (TPST), which adds a sulfate group to the tyrosine residue at the N-terminus of PSGL-1. Which of the following statements best explains the mechanism by which TPST-mediated sulfation enables PSGL-1 binding to P-selectin?

(A) Sulfation of PSGL-1 increases its affinity for the sialic acid residues on P-selectin, forming stable hydrogen bonds.
(B) Sulfation of PSGL-1 alters its conformation, exposing hydrophobic patches that interact with complementary regions on P-selectin.
(C) Sulfation of the N-terminal tyrosine of PSGL-1 creates a negatively charged group that forms an electrostatic coordination complex with a positively charged region on P-selectin.
(D) Sulfation of PSGL-1 prevents its degradation by cellular proteases, allowing it to remain on the neutrophil surface long enough to interact with P-selectin.
(E) Sulfation of PSGL-1 enhances its interaction with integrins on the neutrophil surface, facilitating its transport to the site of P-selectin expression.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Setting:** Biochemical research on leukocyte adhesion.
- **Key Molecules:** P-selectin (on endothelial cells), PSGL-1 (on neutrophils).
- **Observation:** Chlorate (sulfation inhibitor) treatment prevents PSGL-1 binding to P-selectin, despite normal Sialyl Lewis X glycosylation.
- **Enzyme Identified:** Tyrosylprotein sulfotransferase (TPST).
- **Modification Site:** N-terminal tyrosine of PSGL-1.
- **Question:** Mechanism by which TPST-mediated sulfation enables PSGL-1 binding to P-selectin.
- **Key Clue:** The experiment shows that sulfation is *required* for binding, and the modification occurs on the N-terminal tyrosine. This points towards a charge-based interaction. Sialyl Lewis X is known to be the carbohydrate ligand for selectins, but the vignette states sulfation is *also* required, suggesting it plays a distinct, essential role.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Leukocyte Rolling:** The initial step in leukocyte extravasation involves leukocytes loosely adhering to the endothelium, a process called rolling. This is mediated by selectins (E-selectin, P-selectin) on endothelial cells and their ligands (e.g., PSGL-1, CD34) on leukocytes.
- **P-selectin & PSGL-1:** P-selectin is rapidly mobilized to the endothelial surface upon activation (e.g., inflammation, thrombus formation). PSGL-1 is the major ligand for P-selectin on neutrophils.
- **Sialyl Lewis X:** PSGL-1 contains carbohydrate moieties, including Sialyl Lewis X (sLeX), which is the primary recognition site for selectins. sLeX is a tetrasaccharide containing sialic acid, galactose, fucose, and N-acetylglucosamine. The fucose residue is crucial for binding.
- **Role of Sulfation:** While sLeX is necessary for binding, studies have shown that sulfation of the N-terminal tyrosine residue of PSGL-1 is *also* essential for high-affinity binding to P-selectin under physiological flow conditions.
- **TPST Action:** Tyrosylprotein sulfotransferase (TPST) catalyzes the transfer of a sulfate group from 3'-phosphoadenosine-5'-phosphosulfate (PAPS) to the hydroxyl group of the tyrosine residue at the N-terminus of PSGL-1.
- **Mechanism of Sulfation:** The addition of the sulfate group (-SO3H) to the tyrosine residue converts the hydroxyl group (-OH) into a sulfonate group. This sulfonate group is strongly acidic and carries a significant negative charge at physiological pH.
- **Electrostatic Interaction:** The P-selectin molecule contains positively charged amino acid residues (e.g., arginine, lysine) within its lectin domain, which forms the binding site for sLeX. The negatively charged sulfonate group on the N-terminal tyrosine of sulfated PSGL-1 forms a crucial electrostatic coordination complex with these positively charged residues on P-selectin. This electrostatic interaction significantly strengthens the binding affinity and is particularly important under the shear stress conditions of blood flow.
- **Experimental Evidence:** The vignette explicitly states that inhibiting sulfation with chlorate prevents PSGL-1 binding to P-selectin, confirming the necessity of this modification.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. While hydrogen bonds are involved in molecular interactions, the primary role of sulfation in PSGL-1/P-selectin binding is electrostatic, not hydrogen bonding with sialic acid. Sialic acid itself is involved in hydrogen bonding with P-selectin, but sulfation enhances the overall interaction differently.
- (B): Incorrect. While conformational changes can be important in protein-protein interactions, the specific role of TPST-mediated sulfation on PSGL-1 is not primarily to induce a conformational change exposing hydrophobic patches. The key interaction is electrostatic.
- (D): Incorrect. Sulfation does not primarily function to protect PSGL-1 from degradation. While protein stability is important, the direct role of sulfation in this specific interaction is binding affinity, not preventing proteolysis.
- (E): Incorrect. Sulfation occurs on PSGL-1 itself, not on integrins. Integrins are involved in later stages of leukocyte adhesion (firm adhesion), not the initial rolling mediated by selectins and PSGL-1. Sulfation does not enhance integrin interaction.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Post-translational modifications, such as sulfation, are crucial for the function of many proteins, including cell adhesion molecules. TPST-mediated sulfation of PSGL-1 creates a negative charge essential for electrostatic interaction with positively charged residues on P-selectin, enabling leukocyte rolling.
- **Differentials:** Understand the roles of other post-translational modifications (glycosylation, phosphorylation, acetylation, ubiquitination). Recognize the different types of cell adhesion (selectin-mediated rolling, integrin-mediated firm adhesion, cadherin-mediated cell-cell adhesion). Know the key players in leukocyte extravasation (selectins, integrins, PSGL-1, ICAM-1, VCAM-1).

<a id='question-065'></a>

### Question 65: Hodgkin Lymphoma: Reed-Sternberg Cell Marker
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Identification of the characteristic immunohistochemical markers expressed by Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells in classical Hodgkin lymphoma (cHL), specifically CD15 and CD30, and differentiating them from other lymphoid malignancies or reactive processes. (识别霍奇金淋巴瘤中里德-施特恩伯格 (RS) 细胞的特征性免疫组织化学标记物，特别是 CD15 和 CD30，并将其与其他淋巴恶性肿瘤或反应性过程区分开来。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 24-year-old male presents to his primary care physician complaining of a painless lump in his left neck that he noticed about a month ago. He also reports experiencing night sweats and unintentional weight loss of approximately 6 kg over the past two months. His past medical history is unremarkable, and he denies any fever, cough, or other systemic symptoms besides the night sweats and weight loss. Physical examination reveals a firm, non-tender, 2 cm lymph node in the left cervical region. No other lymphadenopathy is palpable. An excisional biopsy of the cervical lymph node is performed. Histopathological examination shows a lymph node architecture effaced by a mixed inflammatory infiltrate consisting of lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils, and histiocytes. Scattered within this background are large, atypical binucleated giant cells with abundant cytoplasm and prominent eosinophilic inclusion-like nucleoli, resembling "owl's eyes". Immunohistochemical staining reveals that these giant cells are positive for CD15 and CD30, and negative for CD45 (leukocyte common antigen) and most B-cell and T-cell markers. Based on these findings, which of the following statements regarding the neoplastic cells in this patient's lymph node is most accurate?

(A) The neoplastic cells are derived from B lymphocytes and express surface immunoglobulin.
(B) The neoplastic cells are derived from T lymphocytes and express CD3 and CD45.
(C) The neoplastic cells are derived from B lymphocytes but have lost expression of CD20 and CD45.
(D) The neoplastic cells are derived from B lymphocytes and express CD15 and CD30, but lack surface immunoglobulin.
(E) The neoplastic cells are derived from B lymphocytes and express CD15 and CD30, and also express CD20 and CD45.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 24-year-old male with painless cervical lymphadenopathy, night sweats, and weight loss (B symptoms). This constellation strongly suggests lymphoma, particularly Hodgkin lymphoma (HL) given the age and B symptoms.
- **Physical Exam:** Firm, non-tender cervical lymph node. Consistent with lymphoma.
- **Histopathology:** Effaced lymph node architecture with mixed inflammatory infiltrate (lymphocytes, plasma cells, eosinophils, histiocytes). This is characteristic of the reactive background seen in classical Hodgkin lymphoma (cHL).
- **Atypical Cells:** Large, binucleated giant cells with prominent eosinophilic inclusion-like nucleoli ("owl's eyes"). These are the pathognomonic Reed-Sternberg (RS) cells of cHL.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** RS cells are positive for CD15 and CD30, and negative for CD45 (LCA) and most B/T cell markers. This immunophenotype is crucial for diagnosing cHL.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the most accurate statement regarding the neoplastic cells (RS cells) based on the findings.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Reed-Sternberg (RS) Cells:** These are the malignant cells in classical Hodgkin lymphoma (cHL). They are derived from germinal center B lymphocytes.
- **Origin:** Although derived from B cells, RS cells typically lose expression of many pan-B-cell markers like CD20 and CD45 (LCA) during their malignant transformation. This loss of B-cell markers can make diagnosis challenging based solely on morphology or initial broad IHC panels.
- **Key Markers:** RS cells characteristically express CD15 and CD30. CD15 (also known as Lewis X antigen or SSEA-1) is a carbohydrate adhesion molecule normally expressed on granulocytes and monocytes, but aberrantly expressed on RS cells. CD30 is a member of the tumor necrosis factor receptor superfamily.
- **Immunoglobulin Expression:** RS cells generally lack surface immunoglobulin (sIg) expression, reflecting their B-cell origin but altered phenotype.
- **CD45 Expression:** RS cells are typically negative for CD45 (Leukocyte Common Antigen), which is expressed on most hematopoietic cells, including normal B and T lymphocytes. This negativity helps distinguish RS cells from reactive lymphocytes within the inflammatory background.
- **Clinical Correlation:** The patient's presentation (young adult, B symptoms, cervical lymphadenopathy) and the histological/immunohistochemical findings (RS cells in a mixed inflammatory background, positive for CD15/CD30, negative for CD45) are classic for classical Hodgkin lymphoma.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Incorrect. While RS cells are derived from B lymphocytes, they typically *lack* surface immunoglobulin expression. They also do not express pan-B cell markers like CD20 or CD45. This option incorrectly states they express surface immunoglobulin.
- (B): Incorrect. RS cells are derived from B lymphocytes, not T lymphocytes. Therefore, they do not express T-cell markers like CD3. They are also typically negative for CD45.
- (C): Incorrect. RS cells are derived from B lymphocytes, but they characteristically *lose* expression of CD20 and CD45, which are pan-B-cell markers. This option incorrectly states they express CD20 and CD45.
- (D): Correct. This option accurately describes the origin (B lymphocytes), the characteristic positive markers (CD15 and CD30), and the lack of surface immunoglobulin expression in RS cells of classical Hodgkin lymphoma.
- (E): Incorrect. RS cells are derived from B lymphocytes, but they typically *lack* expression of CD20 and CD45, which are pan-B-cell markers. This option incorrectly states they express CD20 and CD45.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Reed-Sternberg cells in classical Hodgkin lymphoma are derived from B cells but typically lose pan-B-cell markers (CD20, CD45, sIg) and characteristically express CD15 and CD30.
- **Differentials:** Consider other causes of lymphadenopathy (infection, autoimmune disease, other lymphomas like non-Hodgkin lymphoma, metastatic carcinoma). Distinguishing cHL from other lymphomas often relies on identifying RS cells and their specific immunophenotype (CD15+, CD30+, CD45-, CD20-). Nodular lymphocyte-predominant Hodgkin lymphoma (NLPHL) has a different morphology (L&H cells) and immunophenotype (CD20+, CD15-, CD30-).

<a id='question-066'></a>

### Question 66: Neutrophil Adhesion & Leukocyte Extravasation
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the molecular mechanisms underlying leukocyte adhesion to the endothelium during inflammation, specifically the "inside-out signaling" pathway that activates integrins (like LFA-1) on neutrophils, enabling firm adhesion and subsequent extravasation. (测试炎症过程中白细胞黏附内皮细胞的分子机制，特别是“从内向外”信号通路，该通路激活中性粒细胞上的整合素（如LFA-1），从而实现牢固黏附并随后进行细胞外渗。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male presents to the emergency department with a 3-day history of worsening right lower quadrant abdominal pain, fever (38.5°C), nausea, and anorexia. He has a history of hypertension managed with lisinopril. Physical examination reveals localized tenderness and guarding in the right lower quadrant, with rebound tenderness. Laboratory results show leukocytosis (WBC count 18,000/µL with neutrophilia) and elevated C-reactive protein. An abdominal CT scan confirms acute appendicitis with periappendiceal inflammation. Intravital microscopy of the mesenteric microvasculature near the inflamed appendix reveals neutrophils rolling along the endothelial surface. Upon administration of recombinant human Interleukin-8 (IL-8), these rolling neutrophils rapidly arrest, flatten, and begin to migrate through the endothelial cell junctions into the periappendiceal tissue. Which of the following molecular events is *most directly* responsible for the firm adhesion of neutrophils to the endothelium following IL-8 stimulation?

(A) Activation of Phospholipase A2 leading to the synthesis of Leukotriene B4 (LTB4), which binds to BLT1 receptors on neutrophils, increasing actin polymerization and cell motility.
(B) IL-8 binding to CXCR1/2 receptors on neutrophils, activating Gq proteins, leading to increased intracellular calcium and activation of Protein Kinase C (PKC), which phosphorylates the cytoplasmic tail of integrins.
(C) IL-8 binding to CXCR1/2 receptors on neutrophils, activating Gi proteins, leading to inhibition of adenylyl cyclase and decreased cAMP levels, which promotes the conformational change of integrins from a low-affinity to a high-affinity state.
(D) IL-8 binding to CXCR1/2 receptors on neutrophils, activating Gq proteins, leading to increased intracellular calcium and activation of Rap1 GTPase, which recruits talin and kindlin to the cytoplasmic tail of integrin β-chains, promoting integrin unfolding and high-affinity binding to ICAM-1.
(E) IL-8 binding to CXCR1/2 receptors on neutrophils, activating Gs proteins, leading to activation of adenylyl cyclase and increased cAMP levels, which promotes the dissociation of the β2 subunit of integrins from the α subunit, exposing the ligand-binding site.

#### Correct Answer: D

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- The patient presents with classic signs and symptoms of acute appendicitis (RLQ pain, fever, leukocytosis, CT findings), indicating an inflammatory process.
- Intravital microscopy shows neutrophils rolling on the endothelium, a preliminary step in leukocyte extravasation.
- Administration of IL-8 (CXCL8) causes the rolling neutrophils to arrest and adhere firmly, followed by migration (extravasation).
- The question asks for the *most direct* molecular mechanism responsible for this *firm adhesion* triggered by IL-8. This points towards the activation of integrins on the neutrophil surface.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Leukocyte Extravasation Steps:** The process involves margination, rolling, activation, firm adhesion, and transmigration.
- **Rolling:** Mediated by selectins (E-selectin, P-selectin on endothelium; L-selectin on leukocytes) binding to their ligands (e.g., sialyl-Lewis X).
- **Activation:** Chemokines (like IL-8/CXCL8) presented on the endothelial surface bind to G-protein coupled receptors (GPCRs) on leukocytes (e.g., CXCR1/2 on neutrophils).
- **Firm Adhesion:** This step requires integrins (e.g., LFA-1, Mac-1) on leukocytes to bind with high affinity to their ligands (e.g., ICAM-1, VCAM-1) on the endothelium.
- **Inside-Out Signaling:** The activation of integrins is a classic example of "inside-out signaling." Chemokine binding to GPCRs initiates an intracellular signaling cascade.
    - IL-8 binds to CXCR1/2, a GPCR coupled to Gq proteins.
    - Gq activation leads to activation of Phospholipase C (PLC).
    - PLC hydrolyzes PIP2 into IP3 and DAG.
    - IP3 increases intracellular calcium ([Ca2+]i).
    - DAG activates Protein Kinase C (PKC).
    - Increased [Ca2+]i and PKC activation lead to the activation of Rap1, a small GTPase.
    - Activated Rap1 recruits adaptor proteins like talin and kindlin to the cytoplasmic tail of the integrin β-chains (e.g., β2 subunit of LFA-1).
    - Talin and kindlin bind to the cytoplasmic tail and the transmembrane domain, causing a conformational change in the integrin.
    - This conformational change unfolds the integrin headpiece, exposing the ligand-binding site and transitioning it from a low-affinity to a high-affinity state.
    - The high-affinity integrin (LFA-1) can now bind strongly to its ligand (ICAM-1) on the endothelium, mediating firm adhesion.
- **Transmigration:** Following firm adhesion, integrins like Mac-1 (αMβ2) bind to ICAM-1, and PECAM-1 (CD31) mediates movement between endothelial cells.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): LTB4 is a potent chemoattractant and activator of neutrophils, but its primary role is chemotaxis (motility) rather than the *initial* firm adhesion step mediated by integrin activation. While it contributes to the overall inflammatory response, it doesn't directly explain the "inside-out" signaling cascade leading to integrin activation for firm adhesion. The receptor is BLT1, and the mechanism involves actin polymerization, which is important for migration *after* adhesion.
- (B): This option describes part of the signaling pathway downstream of Gq activation (PLC, Ca2+, PKC), but it incorrectly states that PKC directly phosphorylates the cytoplasmic tail of integrins. While PKC is involved, the key step for integrin activation (the conformational change leading to high affinity) involves Rap1, talin, and kindlin recruitment, not direct phosphorylation by PKC. Phosphorylation does occur but is not the primary driver of the conformational change for high-affinity binding in this context.
- (C): This option describes signaling via Gi proteins, which is incorrect for CXCR1/2. CXCR1/2 are primarily coupled to Gq. Gi activation leads to decreased cAMP, which can have effects on leukocyte function, but it is not the primary pathway for IL-8-mediated integrin activation.
- (E): This option describes signaling via Gs proteins, which is incorrect for CXCR1/2. Gs activation leads to increased cAMP. While some integrin regulation involves cAMP, this is not the mechanism for IL-8-induced activation via CXCR1/2. Furthermore, the described effect (dissociation of β2 subunit) is not the mechanism of activation for LFA-1 (αLβ2) in response to IL-8.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the sequence of leukocyte extravasation (rolling, activation, firm adhesion, transmigration) and the specific molecular players involved, especially the "inside-out signaling" pathway where chemokine binding activates integrins for firm adhesion. Remember that IL-8/CXCR1/2 signaling activates Gq -> PLC -> Ca2+ -> Rap1 -> Talin/Kindlin recruitment -> Integrin conformational

<a id='question-067'></a>

### Question 67: Leukocyte Adhesion & Activation
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The activation of beta-2 integrins (e.g., LFA-1, Mac-1) on leukocytes involves a conformational change from a low-affinity, bent state to a high-affinity, extended state, mediated by intracellular signals (e.g., chemokines, complement fragments), enabling firm adhesion to endothelial cells. (β2整合素的激活涉及从低亲和力弯曲状态到高亲和力延伸状态的构象变化，由细胞内信号介导，从而实现与内皮细胞的牢固粘附。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 62-year-old male with a history of hypertension and type 2 diabetes mellitus presents to the emergency department with acute onset of severe chest pain radiating to his left arm. An electrocardiogram (ECG) shows ST-segment elevation in leads II, III, and aVF, consistent with an acute inferior myocardial infarction. He is immediately taken to the cardiac catheterization laboratory. During the procedure, a coronary angiogram reveals a complete occlusion of the right coronary artery (RCA) due to a ruptured atherosclerotic plaque with superimposed thrombus. The interventional cardiologist performs percutaneous coronary intervention (PCI) with stent placement. Post-procedure, the patient develops signs of systemic inflammatory response, including fever and elevated C-reactive protein (CRP). Microscopic examination of a peripheral blood smear reveals numerous neutrophils. A researcher is studying the mechanism of neutrophil adhesion to the activated endothelium at the site of the plaque rupture. Using cryo-electron microscopy, they observe the extracellular domain of the beta-2 integrin LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18) on resting neutrophils. Upon exposure to a chemokine gradient generated at the site of endothelial injury, the LFA-1 molecule undergoes a structural change. Which of the following best describes the conformational change occurring in LFA-1 that facilitates firm neutrophil adhesion to the endothelium?

(A) The integrin subunits dissociate, exposing cryptic binding sites for selectins.
(B) The integrin headpiece rotates 180 degrees, exposing a novel ligand-binding domain for fibrinogen.
(C) The integrin headpiece transitions from a bent, closed conformation to an extended, open conformation, increasing the affinity for ICAM-1.
(D) The integrin undergoes glycosylation of its extracellular domain, enhancing its interaction with sialyl-Lewis X on endothelial cells.
(E) The integrin translocates from the apical to the basolateral membrane, facilitating interaction with platelets.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** Acute myocardial infarction (MI) due to RCA occlusion by ruptured plaque and thrombus. This sets the stage for inflammation and leukocyte recruitment.
- **Post-PCI Inflammation:** Systemic inflammatory response (fever, elevated CRP) indicates activation of the immune system.
- **Neutrophil Involvement:** Peripheral blood smear shows numerous neutrophils, key players in the inflammatory response to MI.
- **Mechanism Focus:** The question focuses on the molecular mechanism of neutrophil adhesion to the activated endothelium at the site of plaque rupture.
- **Specific Molecule:** LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18), a beta-2 integrin, is specifically mentioned.
- **Stimulus:** Chemokine gradient at the site of endothelial injury.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the conformational change in LFA-1 that facilitates *firm* adhesion.

The key clue is the transition from *resting* to *activated* neutrophils exposed to chemokines, leading to *firm* adhesion. This points directly to the activation mechanism of beta-2 integrins.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Leukocyte Extravasation:** The process involves several steps: rolling, activation, firm adhesion, and transmigration.
- **Rolling:** Mediated by selectins (E-selectin, P-selectin on endothelium; L-selectin on leukocytes) binding to carbohydrate ligands (e.g., sialyl-Lewis X). This is low-affinity and transient.
- **Activation:** Chemokines (e.g., IL-8, CXCL8) presented on the endothelial surface bind to chemokine receptors on the leukocyte surface (e.g., CXCR1, CXCR2).
- **Inside-Out Signaling:** Chemokine receptor activation triggers intracellular signaling cascades (e.g., involving G proteins, phospholipase C, increased intracellular Ca2+, activation of protein kinase C, activation of talin).
- **Integrin Activation:** This signaling leads to a conformational change in beta-2 integrins (LFA-1, Mac-1, VLA-4) on the leukocyte surface.
- **Conformational Change:** In the resting state, the integrin headpiece is in a "bent" or "closed" conformation, with low affinity for its ligands (e.g., ICAM-1, ICAM-2 on endothelial cells). Upon activation, the headpiece transitions to an "extended" or "open" conformation.
- **Affinity Increase:** This conformational change exposes or creates high-affinity binding sites for ligands like ICAM-1. The affinity increases dramatically (e.g., ~1000-fold for LFA-1 binding to ICAM-1).
- **Firm Adhesion:** The high-affinity interaction between activated integrins (e.g., LFA-1) and endothelial ligands (e.g., ICAM-1) mediates firm adhesion, halting the rolling process.
- **Transmigration:** Following firm adhesion, the leukocyte migrates across the endothelium (paracellularly or transcellularly) into the underlying tissue.

Therefore, the conformational change from a bent, closed, low-affinity state to an extended, open, high-affinity state is the crucial step enabling firm adhesion mediated by beta-2 integrins like LFA-1.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Integrin subunits do not dissociate during activation. Dissociation would likely *inhibit* binding. Selectins mediate rolling, not firm adhesion, and their ligands are carbohydrates, not cryptic binding sites exposed by integrin dissociation. The activation mechanism involves conformational change within the existing heterodimer, not dissociation.
- (B): **Incorrect.** The integrin headpiece does not rotate 180 degrees. While the conformation changes significantly, a simple rotation is not the described mechanism. Fibrinogen is a ligand for integrin αIIbβ3 (GPIIb/IIIa) on platelets, not LFA-1 (αLβ2) on neutrophils, and is involved in platelet aggregation, not neutrophil adhesion to endothelium.
- (D): **Incorrect.** Glycosylation is not the primary mechanism of integrin activation. While glycosylation can affect protein function and localization, the activation of beta-2 integrins is driven by intracellular signaling leading to a conformational change. Selectins bind to sialyl-Lewis X, not integrins. Integrins bind to ICAMs.
- (E): **Incorrect.** Integrins are primarily located on the apical (outer) surface of the leukocyte membrane to interact with endothelial cells. Translocation to the basolateral membrane would facilitate interaction with other cells or structures in that region, not the endothelium. Platelet interaction involves different integrins (αIIbβ3) and adhesion molecules.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Understand the "inside-out" signaling pathway activating beta-2 integrins on leukocytes, involving a conformational change from a low-affinity bent state to a high-affinity extended state, crucial for firm adhesion during inflammation. (理解β2整合素的“由内而外”激活信号通路，涉及从低亲和力弯曲状态到高亲和力延伸状态的构象变化，这对于炎症期间的牢固粘附至关重要。)
- **Differentials:** Differentiate the roles of selectins (rolling, low-affinity, carbohydrate ligands) vs. integrins (firm adhesion, high-affinity, protein ligands like ICAM-1). Understand the sequence of leukocyte extravasation steps. Recognize the specific integrins involved (LFA-1, Mac-1) and their ligands (

<a id='question-068'></a>

### Question 68: Leukocyte Adhesion & Inflammation
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of the molecular mechanisms of leukocyte adhesion to the endothelium during inflammation, specifically the role of integrins (LFA-1) and their ligands (ICAM-1) in firm adhesion. (考察炎症过程中白细胞黏附于内皮细胞的分子机制，特别是整合素（LFA-1）及其配体（ICAM-1）在稳固黏附中的作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 28-year-old female presents to the emergency department with severe abdominal pain, fever (39°C), and rebound tenderness, suggestive of acute bacterial peritonitis secondary to a perforated appendix. Laboratory findings show leukocytosis (WBC count 25,000/µL) with a left shift. Microscopic examination of a biopsy specimen from the inflamed peritoneum reveals numerous neutrophils adherent to the endothelial lining of postcapillary venules. Immunofluorescence staining of the tissue section demonstrates that these neutrophils are flattened and immobilized along the apical surface of the venules. The primary molecular bond anchoring these leukocytes to the vessel wall is formed between a heterodimeric beta-2 integrin expressed on the neutrophil surface and an immunoglobulin superfamily ligand expressed on the endothelial cell surface. Which of the following molecular interactions is primarily responsible for this firm adhesion?

(A) Selectin ligands (e.g., sialyl-Lewis X) on leukocytes binding to selectins (e.g., E-selectin) on endothelial cells.
(B) Integrin VLA-4 (CD49d/CD29, α4β1) on leukocytes binding to VCAM-1 (CD106) on endothelial cells.
(C) Integrin LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18, αLβ2) on leukocytes binding to ICAM-1 (CD54) on endothelial cells.
(D) Integrin Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18, αMβ2) on leukocytes binding to fibronectin in the extracellular matrix.
(E) P-selectin on platelets binding to PSGL-1 on leukocytes.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 28yo female, acute abdominal pain, fever, rebound tenderness -> Suggests acute peritonitis, likely bacterial origin.
- **Lab Findings:** Leukocytosis (25,000/µL) with left shift -> Indicates a significant acute bacterial infection and inflammatory response.
- **Microscopy:** Neutrophils adherent to postcapillary venules in inflamed peritoneum -> This is a hallmark of acute inflammation, specifically leukocyte extravasation.
- **Immunofluorescence:** Neutrophils flattened and immobilized along the venule surface -> Describes the process of firm adhesion, a critical step before transmigration.
- **Key Question Stem:** "The primary molecular bond anchoring these leukocytes to the vessel wall is formed between a heterodimeric beta-2 integrin expressed on the neutrophil surface and an immunoglobulin superfamily ligand expressed on the endothelial cell surface." -> This directly points to the specific molecular interaction responsible for firm adhesion. The question asks to identify this interaction.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
The process of leukocyte extravasation during inflammation involves several sequential steps:
1.  **Margination:** Leukocytes move towards the vessel wall.
2.  **Rolling:** Mediated by selectins (E-selectin, P-selectin) on endothelial cells binding to their ligands (e.g., sialyl-Lewis X on glycoproteins) on leukocytes. This interaction is low affinity and transient, causing leukocytes to "roll" along the endothelium.
3.  **Activation:** Chemokines (e.g., IL-8) released at the site of inflammation activate leukocytes, causing a conformational change in integrins.
4.  **Firm Adhesion:** Activated integrins on leukocytes (like LFA-1 and Mac-1) bind with high affinity to their ligands on endothelial cells (like ICAM-1 and VCAM-1). This interaction is strong and stable, causing the leukocytes to firmly adhere and become flattened. LFA-1 (αLβ2 integrin, CD11a/CD18) on neutrophils binds to ICAM-1 (CD54), which is an immunoglobulin superfamily molecule upregulated on endothelial cells by inflammatory cytokines (TNF, IL-1).
5.  **Transmigration (Diapedesis):** Leukocytes squeeze between endothelial cells, mediated by interactions between integrins (like LFA-1) and ligands like ICAM-1, and also involving PECAM-1 (CD31).
6.  **Chemotaxis:** Leukocytes migrate towards the site of injury guided by chemokines.

The vignette describes firm adhesion, specifically mentioning a beta-2 integrin on the leukocyte binding to an immunoglobulin superfamily ligand on the endothelium. This precisely describes the LFA-1 (αLβ2) - ICAM-1 (CD54) interaction.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Selectin-ligand interactions (e.g., E-selectin/sialyl-Lewis X) mediate the *rolling* phase of leukocyte adhesion, not the *firm adhesion* described in the vignette. Rolling is a lower-affinity, transient interaction. The vignette specifically asks for the *primary molecular bond anchoring* the leukocytes, implying firm adhesion.
- (B): VLA-4 (α4β1) on leukocytes binds to VCAM-1 (CD106) on endothelial cells. This interaction also contributes to firm adhesion, particularly for monocytes, lymphocytes, and eosinophils, but it is generally considered less critical than LFA-1/ICAM-1 for neutrophils in acute bacterial inflammation. VCAM-1 expression is often induced later or to a lesser extent than ICAM-1 in this context. The question stem specifically mentions a beta-2 integrin, and LFA-1 is the primary beta-2 integrin involved in neutrophil firm adhesion via ICAM-1.
- (D): Mac-1 (αMβ2) is another beta-2 integrin on leukocytes that binds to ICAM-1. While it contributes to firm adhesion, LFA-1 is often considered the primary mediator for neutrophils. More importantly, this option describes Mac-1 binding to *fibronectin* in the extracellular matrix. This interaction is crucial for leukocyte migration *through* the extracellular matrix *after* transmigration, not for the initial firm adhesion *to the endothelium*.
- (E): P-selectin on platelets binds to PSGL-1 on leukocytes. This interaction is primarily involved in platelet-leukocyte interactions, often seen in conditions like thrombosis or certain inflammatory states, but it is not the primary mechanism for neutrophil adhesion to the endothelium during acute bacterial peritonitis. P-selectin is also expressed on activated endothelial cells, but its primary ligand in this context is typically PSGL-1, not ICAM-1, and it mediates rolling, not firm adhesion.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
The key steps of leukocyte adhesion are rolling (selectins), activation (chemokines), firm adhesion (integrins like LFA-1/ICAM-1, VLA-4/VCAM-1), and transmigration (integrins/PECAM-1). LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18, αLβ2) binding to ICAM-1 (CD54) is the major mechanism for firm neutrophil adhesion to endothelium in acute inflammation. Differentials include VLA-4/VCAM-1 (more important for monocytes/lymphocytes), selectin-mediated rolling, and integrin-ECM interactions (post-transmigration).

<a id='question-069'></a>

### Question 69: Neutrophil Adhesion and Phagocytosis Defect
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) is a leukocyte integrin (β2 integrin) expressed on neutrophils and monocytes that functions as both an adhesion molecule (binding ICAM-1, fibrinogen) and a receptor for complement opsonins (iC3b), mediating firm adhesion and phagocytosis, respectively. (Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) 是中性粒细胞和单核细胞上表达的白细胞整合素 (β2 整合素)，既作为粘附分子 (结合 ICAM-1、纤维蛋白原) 又作为补体阿胶质受体 (iC3b) 起作用，介导牢固粘附和吞噬作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 5-year-old boy with a history of recurrent bacterial infections, including pneumonia and skin abscesses, presents to the emergency department with a high fever and signs of sepsis. His white blood cell count is markedly elevated (leukocytosis), predominantly neutrophils. Peripheral blood smear shows neutrophils with abnormally shaped cytoplasmic granules. An in vitro assay is performed on the patient's neutrophils. The assay demonstrates severely impaired binding of neutrophils to endothelial cells expressing ICAM-1 and significantly reduced phagocytosis of bacteria coated with iC3b. Flow cytometry reveals absent expression of the CD18 subunit on the neutrophil surface. Which of the following molecular mechanisms best explains the observed functional defects in this patient's neutrophils?

(A) Impaired activation of the complement cascade leading to decreased production of iC3b.
(B) Defective synthesis of actin filaments required for phagosome formation.
(C) Failure of the neutrophil integrin Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) to bind both ICAM-1 and iC3b.
(D) Reduced expression of the complement receptor CR1 (CD35) on neutrophils, impairing iC3b binding.
(E) Defective production of reactive oxygen species (ROS) by the NADPH oxidase complex, inhibiting phagocytosis.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 5-year-old boy with recurrent bacterial infections (pneumonia, skin abscesses), high fever, sepsis, leukocytosis (neutrophilia). This points towards a defect in innate immunity, specifically neutrophil function.
- **Peripheral Blood Smear:** Abnormally shaped cytoplasmic granules in neutrophils. This is a characteristic finding in Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency (LAD).
- **In Vitro Assay:** Impaired binding to ICAM-1 (adhesion) and reduced phagocytosis of iC3b-coated bacteria (opsonophagocytosis). This indicates defects in both neutrophil adhesion to endothelium and phagocytosis mediated by complement.
- **Flow Cytometry:** Absent CD18 expression. CD18 is the common beta chain (β2 subunit) for several leukocyte integrins, including Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18), LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18), and p150,95 (CD11c/CD18). Absence of CD18 explains the defects in both adhesion (mediated by Mac-1, LFA-1) and complement receptor function (Mac-1 also binds iC3b).
- **Diagnosis:** The clinical picture and lab findings are classic for Leukocyte Adhesion Deficiency type 1 (LAD-1), caused by mutations in the *ITGB2* gene encoding the CD18 subunit.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the molecular mechanism explaining the functional defects. The defects are impaired adhesion (ICAM-1 binding) and impaired opsonophagocytosis (iC3b binding). Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) mediates both functions.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Initiating Stimulus:** Bacterial infection triggers inflammation and complement activation.
- **Complement Activation:** The complement cascade is activated, leading to the deposition of complement fragments, including iC3b, on the surface of bacteria (opsonization).
- **Neutrophil Recruitment:** Inflammatory signals (e.g., chemokines) cause neutrophils to roll along the endothelium (mediated by selectins). Subsequent activation causes conformational change in integrins like Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) and LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18).
- **Firm Adhesion:** Activated Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) on the neutrophil binds tightly to ICAM-1 (Intercellular Adhesion Molecule-1) expressed on activated endothelial cells, mediating firm adhesion and allowing neutrophils to extravasate into the tissue. Mac-1 can also bind fibrinogen.
- **Phagocytosis:** Neutrophils migrate to the site of infection. Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) on the neutrophil surface also functions as a receptor for iC3b deposited on the surface of opsonized bacteria. Binding of iC3b to Mac-1 triggers phagocytosis.
- **LAD-1 Pathophysiology:** In LAD-1, mutations in the *ITGB2* gene lead to deficient or absent expression of the CD18 subunit. Without CD18, the integrins Mac-1, LFA-1, and p150,95 cannot be properly assembled or expressed on the neutrophil surface.
- **Consequences:**
    - **Impaired Adhesion:** Lack of functional Mac-1 and LFA-1 prevents firm adhesion to endothelium (ICAM-1 binding) and migration to infection sites.
    - **Impaired Phagocytosis:** Lack of functional Mac-1 prevents binding to iC3b-opsonized bacteria, impairing phagocytosis.
    - **Clinical Manifestations:** Recurrent bacterial infections, impaired wound healing, delayed umbilical cord separation, leukocytosis (neutrophils cannot leave circulation effectively).
- **Conclusion:** The absence of CD18 leads to a non-functional Mac-1 integrin, which cannot bind either ICAM-1 (for adhesion) or iC3b (for opsonophagocytosis). This directly explains the observed functional defects.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Impaired activation of the complement cascade leading to decreased production of iC3b. While complement activation is crucial for opsonization, the vignette states that phagocytosis of *iC3b-coated* bacteria is impaired. The problem lies with the *neutrophil's ability to bind* iC3b, not the production of iC3b itself. Furthermore, the adhesion defect (ICAM-1 binding) is not explained by impaired complement activation. The complement system is likely functioning normally, as evidenced by the presence of iC3b on bacteria, but the neutrophil receptor for it is defective.
- (B): Defective synthesis of actin filaments required for phagosome formation. Actin polymerization is essential for phagocytosis (engulfment), but the primary defect here is the *binding* of the opsonized particle to the neutrophil surface via Mac-1. While phagosome formation might be secondarily affected, the core issue is the failure of initial recognition and binding mediated by the Mac-1 receptor. The adhesion defect is also not explained by this option.
- (D): Reduced expression of the complement receptor CR1 (CD35) on neutrophils, impairing iC3b binding. CR1 (CD35) is the primary receptor for C3b and iC3b. However, Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18) also binds iC3b and is crucial for phagocytosis. The vignette specifically points to the absence of CD18, which affects Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18), LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18), and p150,95 (CD11c/CD18). While reduced CR1 expression could impair iC3b binding, it doesn't explain the adhesion defect (ICAM-1 binding), which is mediated by Mac-1 and LFA-1. The absence of CD18 directly impacts Mac

<a id='question-070'></a>

### Question 70: Endothelial Cell Activation in Atherosclerosis
- **Difficulty**: Medium
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of how pro-inflammatory cytokines, specifically TNF-α and IL-1, induce the expression of adhesion molecules like ICAM-1 on endothelial cells, a key step in leukocyte recruitment during inflammation, particularly relevant in atherosclerosis. (考察炎症细胞因子TNF-α和IL-1如何诱导内皮细胞表达粘附分子ICAM-1，这是炎症期间白细胞募集的关键步骤，尤其与动脉粥样硬化相关。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 58-year-old male with a history of hypertension and hyperlipidemia presents to the emergency department complaining of acute onset chest pain radiating to his left arm. An electrocardiogram (ECG) shows ST-segment elevation in leads II, III, and aVF, consistent with an acute inferior myocardial infarction. Coronary angiography reveals a near-complete occlusion of the right coronary artery (RCA) due to a ruptured atherosclerotic plaque. A research study is investigating the molecular mechanisms underlying leukocyte adhesion to the endothelium at the site of plaque rupture. Endothelial cells harvested from the patient's coronary artery are cultured and subjected to various treatments. Baseline expression of ICAM-1 (CD54) is low. However, treatment with either recombinant human TNF-α or recombinant human IL-1 significantly increases ICAM-1 surface expression within 8 hours. This upregulation is mediated by the activation of the NF-κB transcription factor, leading to increased transcription of the *ICAM1* gene. Which of the following statements BEST describes the functional consequence of this ICAM-1 upregulation in the context of the patient's myocardial infarction?

(A) Increased binding of fibrinogen to the damaged endothelium, promoting thrombus formation.
(B) Enhanced activation of platelets via binding to von Willebrand factor (vWF) exposed on the endothelial surface.
(C) Facilitated adhesion of circulating monocytes and neutrophils to the inflamed endothelium, contributing to plaque instability and inflammation.
(D) Decreased expression of thrombomodulin on endothelial cells, leading to reduced protein C activation and a procoagulant state.
(E) Upregulation of tissue factor (TF) expression on endothelial cells, directly initiating the extrinsic coagulation cascade.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Presentation:** 58-year-old male, hypertension, hyperlipidemia, acute chest pain, ECG showing inferior MI (ST elevation in II, III, aVF), RCA occlusion due to ruptured plaque. This establishes the clinical context of acute coronary syndrome secondary to atherosclerosis and plaque rupture.
- **Research Study:** Focuses on molecular mechanisms of leukocyte adhesion in atherosclerosis. Endothelial cells from the patient's coronary artery are used.
- **Key Finding:** Baseline ICAM-1 (CD54) expression is low. Treatment with TNF-α or IL-1 (pro-inflammatory cytokines) significantly increases ICAM-1 surface expression within 8 hours via NF-κB activation. This highlights the inducible nature of ICAM-1 expression in response to inflammation.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the *functional consequence* of this ICAM-1 upregulation in the context of the patient's MI. This requires understanding the role of ICAM-1 in leukocyte trafficking.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Atherosclerosis & Inflammation:** Atherosclerosis is a chronic inflammatory disease. Plaque rupture is a key event leading to acute thrombotic complications like MI.
- **Endothelial Activation:** The damaged endothelium at the site of plaque rupture becomes activated by inflammatory mediators released from dying cells within the plaque, activated platelets, and circulating inflammatory cells.
- **Cytokine Action:** Pro-inflammatory cytokines like TNF-α and IL-1 are potent activators of endothelial cells.
- **NF-κB Pathway:** TNF-α and IL-1 bind to their respective receptors on endothelial cells, triggering intracellular signaling cascades that activate the transcription factor NF-κB.
- **ICAM-1 Upregulation:** Activated NF-κB translocates to the nucleus and increases the transcription of genes encoding adhesion molecules, including ICAM-1 (CD54).
- **ICAM-1 Function:** ICAM-1 is a cell surface glycoprotein expressed on endothelial cells (and other cell types) that acts as a ligand for leukocyte integrins, specifically LFA-1 (CD11a/CD18) and Mac-1 (CD11b/CD18), which are expressed on monocytes, neutrophils, and other leukocytes.
- **Leukocyte Recruitment:** The increased expression of ICAM-1 on the activated endothelium facilitates the firm adhesion of circulating leukocytes (monocytes, neutrophils) to the vessel wall. This is a crucial step in the process of leukocyte extravasation into the subendothelial space.
- **Consequences:** These recruited leukocytes contribute to further inflammation, plaque progression, and instability by releasing reactive oxygen species, proteases, and more inflammatory cytokines. In the context of acute MI, this inflammatory response exacerbates the thrombotic process.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): **Incorrect.** Fibrinogen binding to the damaged endothelium is primarily mediated by exposed subendothelial matrix components like collagen and vWF, and by platelet activation products like P-selectin. While thrombus formation is crucial in MI, ICAM-1's primary role is leukocyte adhesion, not direct fibrinogen binding.
- (B): **Incorrect.** Platelet activation involves binding to vWF (especially under high shear stress) and collagen exposed at the site of endothelial injury. While endothelial activation occurs, ICAM-1 upregulation primarily targets leukocytes, not platelets, and its main function isn't directly related to platelet activation via vWF.
- (D): **Incorrect.** Thrombomodulin is an endothelial cell surface receptor that binds thrombin, forming a complex with protein C. This complex activates protein C, which inactivates factors Va and VIIIa, thus acting as an anticoagulant. Endothelial activation by TNF-α/IL-1 generally *decreases* thrombomodulin expression or function, contributing to a procoagulant state, but this is not the *primary* functional consequence of ICAM-1 upregulation. ICAM-1's role is leukocyte adhesion.
- (E): **Incorrect.** Tissue factor (TF, CD142) is a key initiator of the extrinsic coagulation cascade. While endothelial cells can express TF, especially when activated by inflammatory mediators like TNF-α and IL-1, the *specific* molecule highlighted in the vignette is ICAM-1. The question asks about the consequence of ICAM-1 upregulation, not TF upregulation. TF upregulation leads to thrombin generation, while ICAM-1 upregulation leads to leukocyte adhesion.

#### Educational Objective (USMLE High-Yield Takeaway & Differentials):
- **High-Yield Takeaway:** Pro-inflammatory cytokines (TNF-α, IL-1) induce endothelial cell activation, leading to upregulation of adhesion molecules like ICAM-1 (CD54). ICAM-1 mediates the adhesion of leukocytes (monocytes, neutrophils) expressing integrins (LFA-1, Mac-1) to the endothelium, a critical step in inflammation and atherosclerosis.
- **Differentials:** Other adhesion molecules involved include E-selectin and P-selectin (mediate initial rolling), VCAM-1 (binds VLA-4 on leukocytes), and ligands for leukocyte integrins like vWF. Thrombomodulin is an anticoagulant receptor. Tissue factor initiates coagulation. Fibrinogen binds to exposed matrix and platelet receptors. Understanding the specific roles of these molecules in endothelial activation and leukocyte recruitment is crucial.

### Question 71: Delayed-Type Hypersensitivity Skin Reaction
- **Difficulty**: Hard
- **Core Concept / 重点考点**: The question tests the understanding of leukocyte adhesion molecules involved in the recruitment of mononuclear cells (lymphocytes and monocytes) to sites of inflammation, specifically in the context of delayed-type hypersensitivity (DTH) reactions. It focuses on the interaction between VLA-4 (α4β1 integrin) on leukocytes and VCAM-1 (CD106) on endothelial cells. (考察迟发型过敏反应中单核细胞和淋巴细胞募集到炎症部位的白细胞黏附分子，特别是VLA-4与VCAM-1的相互作用。)

#### Clinical Vignette:
A 35-year-old male healthcare worker with a history of exposure to tuberculosis patients undergoes a routine tuberculin skin test (PPD). Forty-eight hours after intradermal injection of purified protein derivative (PPD), a localized area of induration and erythema develops at the injection site. A punch biopsy of the lesion reveals a dense infiltrate composed primarily of lymphocytes and monocytes, with minimal neutrophils. Immunohistochemical staining of the infiltrating mononuclear cells shows strong expression of the integrin subunit CD29 (β1). Endothelial cells lining the post-capillary venules within the lesion show increased expression of the adhesion molecule CD106. Which of the following molecular interactions is primarily responsible for the selective recruitment of these mononuclear cells into the PPD reaction site?

(A) Selectin ligands (e.g., sialyl-Lewis X) on leukocytes binding to E-selectin on endothelial cells.
(B) The integrin LFA-1 (αLβ2, CD11a/CD18) on leukocytes binding to ICAM-1 (CD54) on endothelial cells.
(C) The integrin VLA-4 (α4β1, CD49d/CD29) on leukocytes binding to VCAM-1 (CD106) on endothelial cells.
(D) The integrin Mac-1 (αMβ2, CD11b/CD18) on leukocytes binding to fibrinogen on the basement membrane.
(E) The integrin αIIbβ3 on platelets binding to von Willebrand factor (vWF) on endothelial cells.

#### Correct Answer: C

#### High-Yield Pathophysiological Explanation:
##### 1. Clinical Vignette Breakdown & Key Clues (题干与题眼拆解)
- **Patient Profile:** 35-year-old healthcare worker, exposed to TB. This sets the context for a potential immune response to TB antigens.
- **Procedure:** Tuberculin skin test (PPD). This is a classic test for delayed-type hypersensitivity (DTH) to *Mycobacterium tuberculosis*.
- **Timing:** 48 hours post-injection. DTH reactions typically peak around 48-72 hours.
- **Clinical Findings:** Localized induration and erythema. These are characteristic signs of a DTH reaction.
- **Biopsy Findings:** Dense infiltrate of lymphocytes and monocytes, minimal neutrophils. This indicates a mononuclear cell-predominant inflammatory response, typical of DTH, which contrasts with the neutrophil-rich response seen in acute inflammation.
- **Immunohistochemistry:** CD29 (β1 integrin subunit) expression on infiltrating mononuclear cells. CD29 is a key component of the VLA-4 integrin (α4β1).
- **Endothelial Cell Expression:** Increased CD106 (VCAM-1) on endothelial cells. VCAM-1 is an adhesion molecule upregulated on endothelial cells during inflammation.
- **Question Stem:** Asks for the primary molecular interaction responsible for the selective recruitment of these mononuclear cells.

These clues point towards the specific adhesion molecule interactions involved in recruiting lymphocytes and monocytes during a DTH reaction. The presence of VLA-4 (CD29) on the infiltrating cells and VCAM-1 (CD106) on the endothelium strongly suggests the VLA-4/VCAM-1 interaction as the key mechanism.

##### 2. Pathophysiological Deep-Dive (病理生理机制深度剖析)
- **Initiation:** PPD contains *M. tuberculosis* antigens. In a sensitized individual (like this healthcare worker), these antigens are processed by antigen-presenting cells (APCs) in the skin.
- **APC Activation & Cytokine Release:** APCs present the antigen to previously sensitized T helper 1 (Th1) cells. Activated Th1 cells release cytokines, primarily interferon-gamma (IFN-γ) and TNF-α.
- **Endothelial Activation:** IFN-γ and TNF-α act on endothelial cells of post-capillary venules, inducing the expression of adhesion molecules, including VCAM-1 (CD106).
- **Leukocyte Rolling:** Initial tethering and rolling of leukocytes (lymphocytes and monocytes) along the endothelium is mediated by selectins (E-selectin, P-selectin) on endothelial cells binding to their ligands (e.g., sialyl-Lewis X) on leukocytes.
- **Leukocyte Activation:** Chemokines (e.g., CCL2/MCP-1, CXCL10/IP-10) released at the site activate the leukocytes. This activation increases the affinity of integrins on the leukocyte surface.
- **Leukocyte Adhesion:** Activated leukocytes express high-affinity integrins. The key integrin on lymphocytes and monocytes involved in binding to VCAM-1 is VLA-4 (α4β1, CD49d/CD29). VCAM-1 (CD106) is upregulated on the activated endothelium. The firm adhesion of VLA-4 on leukocytes to VCAM-1 on endothelial cells is crucial for arresting the rolling leukocytes.
- **Leukocyte Transmigration (Diapedesis):** Following firm adhesion, leukocytes migrate through the endothelial junctions into the tissue, guided by chemotactic gradients. This process involves interactions between integrins like LFA-1 (αLβ2) and Mac-1 (αMβ2) on leukocytes and their ligands (ICAM-1, ICAM-2, fibrinogen) on endothelial cells and the basement membrane.
- **Outcome:** The accumulation of lymphocytes and monocytes at the site leads to the characteristic DTH reaction (induration, erythema) observed 48 hours after PPD injection. The question specifically asks about the *selective recruitment* of these mononuclear cells, which is primarily mediated by the VLA-4/VCAM-1 interaction.

#### Distractor Analysis (全干扰项逐一深度纠错剖析):
- (A): Selectin-mediated rolling (e.g., E-selectin/sialyl-Lewis X) is the *initial* step in leukocyte recruitment, responsible for tethering and slowing down leukocytes. While important, it's not the primary mechanism for *firm adhesion* and *selective recruitment* of mononuclear cells in DTH, which is characterized by the VLA-4/VCAM-1 interaction. This option describes the *first* step, not the specific interaction highlighted by the clues (CD29/CD106).
- (B): LFA-1 (αLβ2) binding to ICAM-1 (CD54) is crucial for firm adhesion of *all* leukocytes (including neutrophils, monocytes, and lymphocytes) and their subsequent transmigration. While involved in the overall process, it's not specific to the selective recruitment of mononuclear cells in DTH, nor is it the interaction specifically indicated by the CD29/CD106 findings. LFA-1 is more broadly involved in leukocyte adhesion.
- (D): Mac-1 (αMβ2) binding to fibrinogen is primarily involved in the adhesion of *monocytes* and *neutrophils* to the basement membrane and endothelial cells, facilitating transmigration. While monocytes express Mac-1, the question highlights the specific interaction between VLA-4 (on both lymphocytes and monocytes) and VCAM-1 (on endothelium) as the key selective recruitment step for mononuclear cells in DTH. VLA-4/VCAM-1 is considered more specific for lymphocyte and monocyte recruitment compared to Mac-1/fibrinogen.
- (E): αIIbβ3 (GPIIb/IIIa) is an integrin found primarily on platelets, involved in platelet aggregation and adhesion to vWF. It is not expressed
